\displaystyle\lesssim\left[1+r_{\pi}(n,\delta)\right](2+\vartheta_{\ell})^{2}\phi_{s_{\ell}(x)}\left[1+\sqrt{\frac{\log(\frac{1}{\delta})+s_{\ell}(x)\log\left(\frac{ed}{s_{\ell}(x)}\right)}{n}}+\frac{\log(\frac{1}{\delta})+s_{\ell}(x)\log\left(\frac{ed}{s_{\ell}(x)}\right)}{n}\right].

Proof of Lemma
E.11
.

We only prove the second statement, as the proof of the first statement is already contained in the following proof. By Assumption
5
, we know that with probability
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\sup_{v\in\mathcal{C}_{2}\left(S_{\ell}(x),\vartheta_{\ell}\right)\cap B_{d}(0,1)}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{\pi}_{i}\left[X_{i}^{T}v\right]^{2}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{v\in\mathcal{C}_{2}\left(S_{\ell}(x),\vartheta_{\ell}\right)\cap B_{d}(0,1)}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\pi_{i}\left[X_{i}^{T}v\right]^{2}\right|+\sup_{v\in\mathcal{C}_{2}\left(S_{\ell}(x),\vartheta_{\ell}\right)\cap B_{d}(0,1)}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\widehat{\pi}_{i}-\pi_{i}\right)\left[X_{i}^{T}v\right]^{2}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left[1+r_{\pi}(n,\delta)\right]\sup_{v\in\mathcal{C}_{2}\left(S_{\ell}(x),\vartheta_{\ell}\right)\cap B_{d}(0,1)}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(X_{i}^{T}v\right)^{2}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left[1+r_{\pi}(n,\delta)\right]\sup_{v\in\mathcal{C}_{2}\left(S_{\ell}(x),\vartheta_{\ell}\right)\cap B_{d}(0,1)}\left[\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\{\left(X_{i}^{T}v\right)^{2}-\mathrm{E}\left[\left(X_{i}^{T}v\right)^{2}\right]\right\}\right|+\left|v^{T}\mathrm{E}\left[X_{1}X_{1}^{T}\right]v\right|\right].

By Lemma
E.7
, we know that

\displaystyle\sup_{v\in\mathcal{C}_{2}\left(S_{\ell}(x),\vartheta_{\ell}\right)\cap B_{d}(0,1)}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\{\left(X_{i}^{T}v\right)^{2}-\mathrm{E}\left[\left(X_{i}^{T}v\right)^{2}\right]\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim(2+\vartheta_{\ell})^{2}\phi_{s_{\ell}(x)}\left[\sqrt{\frac{\log(\frac{1}{\delta})+s_{\ell}(x)\log\left(\frac{ed}{s_{\ell}(x)}\right)}{n}}+\frac{\log(\frac{1}{\delta})+s_{\ell}(x)\log\left(\frac{ed}{s_{\ell}(x)}\right)}{n}\right]

with probability at least
1-\delta
. In addition, we know from Lemma
E.4
that there exists a discrete set
M_{d,\ell}\subset B_{d}(0,1)
such that
\mathcal{C}_{2}\left(S_{\ell}(x),\vartheta_{\ell}\right)\cap B_{d}(0,1)\subset 2(2+\vartheta_{\ell})\cdot\mathrm{conv}(M_{d,\ell})
with
\left|\left|v\right|\right|_{0}\leq s_{\ell}(x)
for all
v\in M_{d,\ell}
. By Lemma
E.5
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\sup_{v\in\mathcal{C}_{2}\left(S_{\ell}(x),\vartheta_{\ell}\right)\cap B_{d}(0,1)}\left|v^{T}\mathrm{E}\left[X_{1}X_{1}^{T}\right]v\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{v\in 2(2+\vartheta_{\ell})\mathrm{conv}(M_{d,\ell})}\left|v^{T}\mathrm{E}\left[X_{1}X_{1}^{T}\right]v\right|

\displaystyle=\sup_{v\in 2(2+\vartheta_{\ell})\cdot M_{d,\ell}}\left|v^{T}\mathrm{E}\left[X_{1}X_{1}^{T}\right]v\right|

\displaystyle\leq 4(2+\vartheta_{\ell})^{2}\phi_{s_{\ell}(x)}.

Therefore, we conclude that

\displaystyle\sup_{v\in\mathcal{C}_{2}\left(S_{\ell}(x),\vartheta_{\ell}\right)\cap B_{d}(0,1)}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{\pi}_{i}\left[X_{i}^{T}v\right]^{2}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left[1+r_{\pi}(n,\delta)\right](2+\vartheta_{\ell})^{2}\phi_{s_{\ell}(x)}\left[\sqrt{\frac{\log(\frac{1}{\delta})+s_{\ell}(x)\log\left(\frac{ed}{s_{\ell}(x)}\right)}{n}}+\frac{\log(\frac{1}{\delta})+s_{\ell}(x)\log\left(\frac{ed}{s_{\ell}(x)}\right)}{n}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+4(2+\vartheta_{\ell})^{2}\phi_{s_{\ell}(x)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left[1+r_{\pi}(n,\delta)\right](2+\vartheta_{\ell})^{2}\phi_{s_{\ell}(x)}\left[1+\sqrt{\frac{\log(\frac{1}{\delta})+s_{\ell}(x)\log\left(\frac{ed}{s_{\ell}(x)}\right)}{n}}+\frac{\log(\frac{1}{\delta})+s_{\ell}(x)\log\left(\frac{ed}{s_{\ell}(x)}\right)}{n}\right]

with probability at least
1-\delta
. The result follows.
∎

Lemma E.12
.

Let
\delta\in(0,1)
and
x
be a fixed covariate vector. Suppose that Assumptions
1
,
2
and
4
hold. Then, with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\left|\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widetilde{Z}_{i}X_{i}X_{i}^{T}\ell_{0}(x)+2x\right|\right|_{\infty}\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{\phi_{1}}}{\kappa_{R}}\left|\left|x\right|\right|_{2}\left[\sqrt{\frac{\log(d/\delta)}{n}}+\frac{\log(d/\delta)}{n}\right],

where the above statement holds when
\widetilde{Z}_{i}=\pi(X_{i}),i=1,...,n
or
\widetilde{Z}_{i}=R_{i},i=1,...,n
. If, in addition, Assumption
5
holds, then with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\left|\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{\pi}_{i}X_{i}X_{i}^{T}\ell_{0}(x)+2x\right|\right|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{\phi_{1}}}{\kappa_{R}}\left|\left|x\right|\right|_{2}\left[\sqrt{\frac{\log(d/\delta)}{n}}+\frac{\log(d/\delta)}{n}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\sqrt{\phi_{1}\phi_{s_{\ell}(x)}}\left|\left|x\right|\right|_{2}}{\kappa_{R}^{2}}\left[1+\sqrt{\frac{\log(d/\delta)}{n}}+\frac{\log(d/\delta)}{n}\right]r_{\pi}(n,\delta).

Proof of Lemma
E.12
.

We only prove the second statement, as the proof of the first statement is already contained in the following proof. Notice that

\displaystyle\left|\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{\pi}_{i}X_{i}X_{i}^{T}\ell_{0}(x)+2x\right|\right|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\left|\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\pi_{i}X_{i}X_{i}^{T}\ell_{0}(x)+2x\right|\right|_{\infty}}_{\textbf{Term I}}+\underbrace{\left|\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\widehat{\pi}_{i}-\pi_{i}\right)X_{i}X_{i}^{T}\ell_{0}(x)\right|\right|_{\infty}}_{\textbf{Term II}}.

\bullet

Term I:
Recall from (
19
) that
\ell_{0}(x):=-2\left[\mathrm{E}\left(RXX^{T}\right)\right]^{-1}x=-2\left[\mathrm{E}\left(\pi(X)XX^{T}\right)\right]^{-1}x
and thus,

\displaystyle\left|\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\pi(X_{i})X_{i}X_{i}^{T}\ell_{0}(x)+2x\right|\right|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\max_{1\leq k\leq d}\left|-\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\pi(X_{i})X_{ik}X_{i}^{T}\mathrm{E}\left[\pi(X_{i})X_{i}X_{i}^{T}\right]^{-1}x+2x_{k}\right|,

and all the random variables

2x_{1}-\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\pi(X_{i})\cdot X_{i1}X_{i}^{T}\mathrm{E}\left[\pi(X_{i})X_{i}X_{i}^{T}\right]^{-1}x,\dots,2x_{d}-\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\pi(X_{i})\cdot X_{id}X_{i}^{T}\mathrm{E}\left[\pi(X_{i})X_{i}X_{i}^{T}\right]^{-1}x

have zero means and sub-exponential tails (but neither identically distributed nor independent). By Assumption
2
and Lemma
E.13
, we know that

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq k\leq d}\left|\left|x_{k}-\pi(X_{i})\cdot X_{ik}X_{i}^{T}\mathrm{E}\left[\pi(X_{i})X_{i}X_{i}^{T}\right]^{-1}x\right|\right|_{\psi_{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\max_{1\leq k\leq d}\left|\left|\pi(X_{i})\cdot X_{ik}X_{i}^{T}\mathrm{E}\left[\pi(X_{i})X_{i}X_{i}^{T}\right]^{-1}x\right|\right|_{\psi_{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\max_{1\leq k\leq d}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\left|\left|X_{ik}\right|\right|_{\psi_{2}}\left|\left|\pi(X_{i})X_{i}^{T}\mathrm{E}\left[\pi(X_{i})X_{i}X_{i}^{T}\right]^{-1}x\right|\right|_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{\phi_{1}}}{\kappa_{R}}\left|\left|x\right|\right|_{2}

for all
1\leq i\leq n
. Therefore, by the union bound and Bernstein’s inequality (Corollary 2.8.3 in
Vershynin 2018
), we know that for all
t>0
, there exists an absolute constant
A_{1}>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}\left(\left|\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\pi(X_{i})X_{i}X_{i}^{T}\ell_{0}(x)+2x\right|\right|_{\infty}>t\right)

\displaystyle\leq d\cdot\max_{1\leq k\leq d}\mathrm{P}\left(\left|\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[\pi(X_{i})\cdot X_{ik}X_{i}^{T}\mathrm{E}\left[\pi(X_{i})X_{i}X_{i}^{T}\right]^{-1}x-x_{k}\right]\right|>t\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2d\cdot\exp\left(-nA_{1}\min\left\{\frac{t\kappa_{R}}{\sqrt{\phi_{1}}\left|\left|x\right|\right|_{2}},\,\frac{t^{2}\kappa_{R}^{2}}{\phi_{1}\left|\left|x\right|\right|_{2}^{2}}\right\}\right).

Hence, we conclude that with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\left|\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\pi(X_{i})X_{i}X_{i}^{T}\ell_{0}(x)+2x\right|\right|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{\phi_{1}}}{\kappa_{R}}\left|\left|x\right|\right|_{2}\left[\sqrt{\frac{\log(d/\delta)}{n}}+\frac{\log(d/\delta)}{n}\right].

\bullet

Term II:
Note that under Assumption
5
,

\displaystyle\left|\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\widehat{\pi}_{i}-\pi_{i}\right)X_{i}X_{i}^{T}\ell_{0}(x)\right|\right|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\max_{1\leq k\leq d}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\widehat{\pi}_{i}-\pi_{i}\right)X_{ik}X_{i}^{T}\ell_{0}(x)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim r_{\pi}(n,\delta)\cdot\max_{1\leq k\leq d}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|X_{ik}X_{i}^{T}\ell_{0}(x)\right|\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim r_{\pi}(n,\delta)\left[\max_{1\leq k\leq d}\mathrm{E}\left|X_{ik}X_{i}^{T}\ell_{0}(x)\right|+\max_{1\leq k\leq d}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(\left|X_{ik}X_{i}^{T}\ell_{0}(x)\right|-\mathrm{E}\left|X_{ik}X_{i}^{T}\ell_{0}(x)\right|\Big)\right|\right]

with probability at least
1-\delta
. By Lemma
E.8
, we know that
\ell_{0}(x)\in\mathcal{C}_{2}(S_{\ell}(x),\vartheta_{\ell})
with
\vartheta_{\ell}=\frac{r_{\ell}}{\sqrt{1-r_{\ell}^{2}}}
. Hence, by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq k\leq d}\mathrm{E}\left|X_{ik}X_{i}^{T}\ell_{0}(x)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{1\leq k\leq d}\sqrt{\mathrm{E}X_{ik}^{2}}\cdot\sqrt{\mathrm{E}\left[(X_{i}^{T}\ell_{0}(x))^{2}\right]}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\phi_{1}}\cdot\sqrt{\sup_{v\in\mathcal{C}_{2}(S_{\ell}(x),\vartheta_{\ell})\cap\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\mathrm{E}\left[(X_{i}^{T}v)^{2}\right]}\cdot\left|\left|\ell_{0}(x)\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(i)}}}{{\lesssim}}\sqrt{\phi_{1}}\cdot\sqrt{\sup_{v\in 2(2+\vartheta_{\ell})\cdot\mathrm{conv}(M_{d,\ell})}\mathrm{E}\left[(X_{i}^{T}v)^{2}\right]}\cdot\frac{\left|\left|x\right|\right|_{2}}{\kappa_{R}^{2}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(ii)}}}{{\asymp}}\sqrt{\phi_{1}}\cdot\sqrt{\sup_{v\in M_{d,\ell}}\mathrm{E}\left[(X_{i}^{T}v)^{2}\right]}\cdot\frac{\left|\left|x\right|\right|_{2}}{\kappa_{R}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\phi_{1}\phi_{s_{\ell}(x)}}\cdot\frac{\left|\left|x\right|\right|_{2}}{\kappa_{R}^{2}},

where we argue by Lemma
E.4
in the (asymptotic) inequality (i) that there exists a discrete set
M_{d,\ell}\subset B_{d}(0,1)
with cardinality
|M_{d,\ell}|\leq\frac{3}{2}\left(\frac{5ed}{s_{\ell}(x)}\right)^{s_{\ell}(x)}
such that
\mathcal{C}_{1}(S_{\ell}(x),3)\cap\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\subset 10\cdot\mathrm{conv}(M_{d,\ell})
and
\left|\left|u\right|\right|_{0}\leq s_{\ell}(x)
for all
u\in M_{d,\ell}
. Also, in the asymptotic equality (ii), we leverage Lemma
E.5
to show that the supremum of a convex function on
\mathrm{conv}(M_{d,\ell})
is attained at its vertices, which are contained in
M_{d,\ell}
.

Now, we also know that all the random variables

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big[\left|X_{i1}X_{i}^{T}\ell_{0}(x)\right|-\mathrm{E}\left|X_{i1}X_{i}^{T}\ell_{0}(x)\right|\Big],\,...,\,\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big[\left|X_{id}X_{i}^{T}\ell_{0}(x)\right|-\mathrm{E}\left|X_{id}X_{i}^{T}\ell_{0}(x)\right|\Big]

have zero means and sub-exponential tails (but neither identically distributed nor independent). By Assumption
2
and Lemma
E.13
, we know that for all
1\leq i\leq n
,

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq k\leq d}\left|\left|\left|X_{ik}X_{i}^{T}\ell_{0}(x)\right|-\mathrm{E}\left|X_{ik}X_{i}^{T}\ell_{0}(x)\right|\right|\right|_{\psi_{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\max_{1\leq k\leq d}\left|\left|X_{ik}X_{i}^{T}\mathrm{E}\left[\pi(X_{i})X_{i}X_{i}^{T}\right]^{-1}x\right|\right|_{\psi_{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\max_{1\leq k\leq d}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\left|\left|X_{ik}\right|\right|_{\psi_{2}}\left|\left|X_{i}^{T}\mathrm{E}\left[\pi(X_{i})X_{i}X_{i}^{T}\right]^{-1}x\right|\right|_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{\phi_{1}\phi_{s_{\ell}(x)}}}{\kappa_{R}^{2}}\left|\left|x\right|\right|_{2}.

Therefore, by the union bound and Bernstein’s inequality (Corollary 2.8.3 in
Vershynin 2018
), we know that there exists an absolute constant
A_{2}>0
such that for all
t>0
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}\left(\max_{1\leq k\leq d}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(\left|X_{ik}X_{i}^{T}\ell_{0}(x)\right|-\mathrm{E}\left|X_{ik}X_{i}^{T}\ell_{0}(x)\right|\Big)\right|>t\right)

\displaystyle\leq d\cdot\max_{1\leq k\leq d}\mathrm{P}\left(\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(\left|X_{ik}X_{i}^{T}\ell_{0}(x)\right|-\mathrm{E}\left|X_{ik}X_{i}^{T}\ell_{0}(x)\right|\Big)\right|>t\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2d\cdot\exp\left(-nA_{2}\min\left\{\frac{t\kappa_{R}^{2}}{\sqrt{\phi_{1}\phi_{s_{\ell}(x)}}\left|\left|x\right|\right|_{2}},\,\frac{t^{2}\kappa_{R}^{4}}{\phi_{1}\phi_{s_{\ell}(x)}\left|\left|x\right|\right|_{2}^{2}}\right\}\right).

Hence, we conclude that with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq k\leq d}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(\left|X_{ik}X_{i}^{T}\ell_{0}(x)\right|-\mathrm{E}\left|X_{ik}X_{i}^{T}\ell_{0}(x)\right|\Big)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{\phi_{1}\phi_{s_{\ell}(x)}}}{\kappa_{R}^{2}}\left|\left|x\right|\right|_{2}\left[\sqrt{\frac{\log(d/\delta)}{n}}+\frac{\log(d/\delta)}{n}\right]

Therefore, we conclude that with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\textbf{Term II}&=\left|\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\widehat{\pi}_{i}-\pi_{i}\right)X_{i}X_{i}^{T}\ell_{0}(x)\right|\right|_{\infty}\\
&\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{\phi_{1}\phi_{s_{\ell}(x)}}\left|\left|x\right|\right|_{2}}{\kappa_{R}^{2}}\left[1+\sqrt{\frac{\log(d/\delta)}{n}}+\frac{\log(d/\delta)}{n}\right]r_{\pi}(n,\delta).\end{split}

(63)

Combining the bounds for
Term I
and
Term II
yields that with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\left|\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{\pi}_{i}X_{i}X_{i}^{T}\ell_{0}(x)+2x\right|\right|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{\phi_{1}}}{\kappa_{R}}\left|\left|x\right|\right|_{2}\left[\sqrt{\frac{\log(d/\delta)}{n}}+\frac{\log(d/\delta)}{n}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\sqrt{\phi_{1}\phi_{s_{\ell}(x)}}\left|\left|x\right|\right|_{2}}{\kappa_{R}^{2}}\left[1+\sqrt{\frac{\log(d/\delta)}{n}}+\frac{\log(d/\delta)}{n}\right]r_{\pi}(n,\delta).

The proof is completed.
∎

Lemma E.13

(Orlicz-norm of products of random variables)
.

Let
X_{1},...,X_{N}
be random variables with finite
\psi_{\alpha}
-Orlicz norm (
2
). Then, for any
N\geq 1
,
\left|\left|\prod_{i=1}^{N}X_{i}\right|\right|_{\psi_{\frac{\alpha}{N}}}\leq\prod_{i=1}^{N}\left|\left|X_{i}\right|\right|_{\psi_{\alpha}}
.

Proof of Lemma
E.13
.

The result generalizes Lemma 2.7.7 in
Vershynin 2018
, which follows from the Young’s inequality as:
\prod_{i=1}^{N}x_{i}^{\lambda_{i}}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{N}\lambda_{i}x_{i}
for all
x_{i},\lambda_{i}\geq 0,i=1,...,N
with
\sum_{i=1}^{N}\lambda_{i}=1
. Without the loss of generality, we assume that
\left|\left|X_{i}\right|\right|_{\psi_{\alpha}}=1
for all
i=1,...,N
. Then,

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}\left[\exp\left(\prod_{i=1}^{N}|X_{i}|^{\frac{\alpha}{N}}\right)\right]\leq\mathrm{E}\left[\exp\left(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}|X_{i}|^{\alpha}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathrm{E}\left[\prod_{i=1}^{N}\exp\left(\frac{1}{N}|X_{i}|^{\alpha}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathrm{E}\left[\exp\left(|X_{i}|^{\alpha}\right)\right]\leq 2,

where we apply the Young’s inequality to obtain the first and last inequalities.
∎

Lemma E.14

(
Giessing 2022
)
.

Let
\mathcal{F}\subset L_{1}(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{A},\mathrm{P})
and
\varrho
be a pseudo-metric on
\mathcal{F}\times\mathcal{F}
. For
\eta>0
, we define
\mathcal{F}_{\eta}=\left\{f-g:f,g\in\mathcal{F},\varrho(f,g)\leq\eta\right\}
. Suppose that for
\alpha\in(0,2]
, there exists a constant
K>0
such that for all
f,g\in\mathcal{F}
,

\left|\left|\left(f-\mathrm{P}f\right)-\left(g-\mathrm{P}g\right)\right|\right|_{\mathrm{P},\psi_{\alpha}}\leq K\cdot\varrho(f,g).

Then, the following statements hold true:

(a)

If
\alpha\in(0,1]
, then for all
t>0
, with probability at least
1-e^{-t}
,

\displaystyle\left|\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\right|\right|_{\mathcal{F}_{\eta}}\leq C_{\alpha}K\left[\int_{0}^{\eta}\sqrt{\log N(\epsilon,\mathcal{F},\varrho)}\,d\epsilon+n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\int_{0}^{\eta}\big[\log N(\epsilon,\mathcal{F},\varrho)\big]^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}d\epsilon+\eta\left(\sqrt{t}+n^{-\frac{1}{2}}t^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}\right)\right],

where
C_{\alpha}>0
is a constant depending only on
\alpha
.

(b)

If
\alpha\in(1,2]
, then for all
t>0
, with probability at least
1-e^{-t}
,

\displaystyle\left|\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\right|\right|_{\mathcal{F}_{\eta}}\leq C_{\alpha}K\left[\int_{0}^{\eta}\sqrt{\log N(\epsilon,\mathcal{F},\varrho)}\,d\epsilon+n^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{\beta}}\int_{0}^{\eta}\big[\log N(\epsilon,\mathcal{F},\varrho)\big]^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}d\epsilon+\eta\left(\sqrt{t}+n^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{\beta}}t^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}\right)\right],

where
C_{\alpha}>0
is a constant depending only on
\alpha
and
\frac{1}{\alpha}+\frac{1}{\beta}=1
.

Remark E.4

(
Giessing and Wang 2023
)
.

If
\mathcal{F}=\{f_{t}:t\in T\}
and
\left|\left|\left(f_{s}-\mathrm{P}f_{s}\right)-\left(f_{t}-\mathrm{P}f_{t}\right)\right|\right|_{\mathrm{P},\psi_{\alpha}}\leq K\cdot\varrho(s,t)
for all
f_{s},f_{t}\in\mathcal{F}
and a pseudo-metric
\varrho
on
T\times T
, where
K>0
is some constant, then we can replace
N(\epsilon,\mathcal{F},\varrho)
by
N(\epsilon,T,\varrho)
.
